# Master Tracker — Beep.OilandGas.Desktop

**Document ID:** PLAN-00
**Version:** 1.0
**Created:** 2026-11-05
**Owner:** Desktop programme
**Status:** STG-00, STG-01, STG-02, STG-03, STG-03a, STG-04, STG-05, STG-06, STG-07, STG-08, STG-09, STG-10, STG-11, STG-12, STG-13, STG-14, STG-15, STG-16, STG-17 and STG-18 gate met (130 of 130 features gate met) - see section 4. Five acceptance items remain the owner’s, not code: INT-02 AC-2 (Energistics XSDs), INT-03 AC-1 (OSDU JSON schemas), REL-01 AC-1 (a clean Windows VM), REL-03 AC-2/AC-6 (a screen reader and 200 % scaling), and REL-05’s thirty attestations.

---

## 1. Purpose of this document

This is the single source of truth for the desktop programme. It answers four questions:

1. What stages exist, and which one are we in?
2. What features exist, where is each one planned, and what is its status?
3. Which source project in `Beep.OilandGas` or `OilandGasTools` does each feature consume?
4. What must be true before a stage or the programme can be called complete?

Feature-level detail lives in `plan/features/<AREA>/<ID>-<slug>.md`.
Stage-level detail lives in `plan/stages/STG-<NN>-<slug>.md`.

**Rule:** before any implementation or test work, read this tracker and the active stage
document. Do not run broad test suites; select the exact checks named by the active stage or
feature document.

---

## 2. Status legend

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `Not started` | No work done. |
| `In progress` | Work has begun; not gate-tested. |
| `Blocked` | Waiting on a decision (see `04-DECISIONS.md`) or another stage. |
| `Gate met` | Stage exit gate passed with recorded evidence. |
| `Deferred` | Deliberately excluded from the current release; reason recorded. |

Feature status values: `Not started`, `In progress`, `Gate met`, `Deferred`.

## 4. The fifteen rules this programme keeps

Derived from `AGENTS.md`, the ERP reference implementation, and both source repositories.

1. **The desktop never opens a business database connection from a view or view model.** All
   business data goes through `IBeepOilandGasApp` (`Beep.OilandGas.Client`) or a typed
   `I<Area>Service` from `Beep.OilandGas.Client.App.Services`. Database and driver
   administration screens are the only exception, and they use the admin-side connection
   service.
2. **No business logic is re-implemented.** Calculation, module setup, workflow and persistence
   behaviour stay in `Beep.OilandGas.*`. The desktop calls it.
3. **The owning project owns its models.** The desktop defines view models and desktop-only
   presentation records; it never re-declares an entity, DTO or enum that an owning project
   already defines.
4. **One page catalog.** Every navigable page is declared once in `OgModuleCatalog`. The shell
   builds navigation from it and the route guard refuses anything it does not contain — the same
   contract as `ErpModuleCatalog` / `ErpNavigationService`.
5. **Two independent gates per page.** Commercial entitlement (`OgModuleKeys`) *and* the
   signed-in roles/permissions. A page must pass both.
6. **Authorization lives in the client app.** Roles come from the app's own RBAC store
   (`Beep.OilandGas.Repository` / `Beep.OilandGas.UserManagement`), not from IdentityServer.
7. **One theme source.** `BeepChrome` design-system skins + a single token dictionary. No
   page-local palettes, no hardcoded colours in XAML.
8. **One pipeline, no swallowed exception.** Logs, exceptions and audit messages all arrive at one
   service. Every `catch` either reports through
   `IFailureReporter.ReportHandled(exception, operation, consequence, severity)` or rethrows.
   Enforced by analyzers `BEEP0001..BEEP0004` plus the guard tests in
   [`07-LOGGING-AUDIT-AND-NOTIFICATIONS.md`](07-LOGGING-AUDIT-AND-NOTIFICATIONS.md) §6.
9. **Every user-visible message is a toast from that pipeline.** The toast carries a failure
   reference, never exception text. Feature code never injects `IOgToastService`; only the
   notification hub does. Every screen also has an inline failed state with a copyable reference.
10. **Every writing command writes exactly one audit row**, and every refusal is audited too.
11. **Every long operation is cancellable and reports progress.** No blocking UI thread work.
12. **Every screen has five states:** empty, loading, failed, denied, unsaved/recovery.
13. **SkiaSharp interop is confined to `Controls` and `WellTools`.** View models never hold an
    `SKCanvas`, `SKBitmap` or `SKColor`.
14. **`OilandGasTools` views are hosted, not rewritten.** `ViewSurface` is the contract; the
    WPF adapter forwards paint, pointer, wheel, key, pinch and two-finger pan.
15. **Documentation and evidence precede claims.** A stage or feature is only `Gate met` when
    the exact checks in its document were run and the output was recorded.

| RES-01 | Gas properties | 06 | `Beep.OilandGas.GasProperties` | Gate met - a run computes Z, viscosity, density, formation volume factor and pseudo-pressure from the owning project's calculators, with the correlation named beside every result and the same inputs always giving the same answer. At the reference point (specific gravity 0.65, Ppr 3.0, Tpr 1.5) it reproduces **Z = 0.7761** within 2%, cross-checked against the engine's own Brill-Beggs correlation which returns **0.7765** there. A calculation the engine cannot complete is a failed run carrying its reason, never a number. Units are converted in one place and both the typed value and the converted psia/Rankine pair are recorded with every saved run, together with the correlations and the actor. The reference case (35 API, gas gravity 0.65, 200 degF, Rs 600 scf/STB) reproduces **Pb 2896.78 psia**, **Rs 625.56 scf/STB** and **Bo 1.32759 rb/STB** within half a percent of values worked out by hand from the published Standing and Beggs-Robinson relations. A result outside the correlation's stated range is returned and **marked**, not refused; a gas gravity is **never applied silently** (typed, or the default accepted explicitly and recorded as accepted); and a pressure below the computed bubble point is refused naming both numbers. The owner's `OIL_PROPERTY_RESULT` was **extended additively** with the inputs, Pb, Rs, dead viscosity, the assumed-default flag and the range mark rather than adding a parallel table, so the entity count stays 387. A run the solver did not converge is **failed with its iteration count and no composition** (SG-06-3, SG-06-4); the mole fractions must sum to one within the engine's own 0.0001 tolerance; the EOS comes from `FlashEquationOfStateMapping`; the envelope exposes **only computed points** and counts what it left out; and the same inputs give the same output. ### 6.8 Well performance and lift — `LFT`

Both correlations are recorded on every study (AC-5), and the **tubing sweep reports the points it completed** even when cancelled (AC-6). `NODAL_ANALYSIS_RESULT` gained the study columns (both correlations, convergence, binding constraint, diagnostics, inputs, cross-check, actor) - entity count stays 392. Valve spacing is **reproducible** for the chosen method, with the first valve unloading, the last operating, and the closing pressure the standard ratio below the opening one (AC-3). **Port sizes come from the reference set** (AC-4), the units are explicit (AC-5), and the design stores **the method and all six limits evaluated** (AC-6). `GAS_LIFT_DESIGN` gained the method, port, units, valve list, limit columns and actor - entity count stays 392. Route `gaslift` (en + ar). 8 unit + 1 opt-in SQLite test; 958/958 desktop, 41/41 gateway, build 0 errors, smoke `result: ok`, 65 pages, 392 entity types. **Engine fixes:** three gas-lift calculators passed **Fahrenheit into a Rankine correlation**, so every ordinary well was refused; the potential analysis also divided by a zero production rate. |
| LFT-03 | ESP and pump performance | 07 | `Beep.OilandGas.PumpPerformance` | Gate met - a duty point is checked against the pump's published H-Q curve with the owner's calculations: the head is interpolated from the curve, the efficiency comes from the curve's own column (filled from the published power), and the brake horsepower and kilowatts are `PowerCalculations`'. **A duty rate outside the curve is refused with the range it covers** (AC-1, **SG-07-4**). The **NPSH margin** is compared against `PumpConstants.DefaultNpshSafetyMarginFeet` - no literal in the screen - and a thin margin warns with both values (AC-2). An **affinity** result outside the curve is **labelled extrapolated** (AC-3). Every result states its **unit** (AC-5), and a saved design records the **curve source and stage count** (AC-6). New `PUMP_DESIGN`, and a new `PumpPerformanceModule` that also registers the five **previously unregistered** ESP tables (smoke: 29 modules, 398 entity types). Route `pumps` (en + ar). 8 unit + 1 opt-in SQLite test; 967/967 desktop, 42/42 gateway, build 0 errors, smoke `result: ok`, 66 pages. |
| LFT-04 | Sucker rod pumping | 07 | `Beep.OilandGas.SuckerRodPumping` | Gate met - the string is checked with the module's own `SuckerRodLoadCalculator` and `FatigueAnalysis`: peak and minimum polished-rod load, fluid load, maximum stress, the modified-Goodman ratio and the safety factor per section. **An unsafe string is refused and the limiting section is named** with its diameter, stress and Goodman ratio (**AC-1**, **SG-07-5**) - a 0.625 in string over 5,000 ft is refused, and the same string in grade HY passes. The **grades, diameters, stroke lengths and SPM come from `SuckerRodConstants`** (AC-2), and a source guard keeps the grade names out of the view. The **pump card** is produced and its extremes match the computed loads (AC-3); the design is **reproducible** (AC-4); every length and diameter carries **its unit** (AC-5); and a saved design records the **grade, the taper and the safety factor achieved** (AC-6). New `SRP_DESIGN` and a new `SuckerRodPumpingModule` (smoke: 30 modules, 399 entity types). Route `suckerrod` (en + ar). 9 unit + 1 opt-in SQLite test; 977/977 desktop, 43/43 gateway, build 0 errors, smoke `result: ok`, 67 pages. |
| LFT-05 | Plunger lift | 07 | `Beep.OilandGas.PlungerLift` | Gate met - **six criteria are evaluated and printed with their deciding value and the module's own range** (AC-1): GLR, pressure differential, fall velocity, rise velocity, cycle time and slug size. **An infeasible well reports no rate at all** - not a zero - and cannot be saved as a design (AC-2), while a **feasible but uneconomic** well is a **third state** that reports the rate the cycle can lift and the basis that made it uneconomic (AC-3): **SG-07-6 has two distinct halves**. The **types come from `PlungerLiftConstants`** with a source guard that the view names none (AC-4); the **fall, rise and shut-in times add up to the cycle**, and the cycle count and daily rate follow from it (AC-5); and a saved assessment records the **type, the cycle, the verdict and every criterion** (AC-6). New `PLUNGER_DESIGN` and a new `PlungerLiftModule` (smoke: 31 modules, 400 entity types). Route `plunger` (en + ar). 11 unit + 1 opt-in SQLite test; 989/989 desktop, 44/44 gateway, build 0 errors, smoke `result: ok`, 68 pages. **Engine fix:** the module's rise-velocity limits made **every real well infeasible** - the estimate depends on the differential alone, so 50 psi gives exactly 500 ft/min against a 500 maximum while the same 50 psi is the minimum differential; the limits now state the calculator's own documented 300-1200 ft/min clamp. |
| LFT-06 | Hydraulic pumps | 07 | `Beep.OilandGas.HydraulicPumps` | Gate met - the jet and piston designs come from the module's own calculators, and a reference jet design **reproduces the module's M x N efficiency** recomputed independently from its own reported numbers (AC-1). **Cavitation is reported with its index and threshold**: available NPSH, required NPSH and the margin, agreeing with the module's own boolean check (AC-2). A **clamped efficiency is a warning carrying both numbers**, not a silent answer (AC-3) - which needed `TryCalculateJetPumpPerformance`, added beside the unchanged `CalculateJetPumpPerformance`. **Every finding states its severity** (Info/Warning/Error) from `HydraulicPumpFindings`, a new owner catalog whose sentences are the validator's own (a test asserts one message equals the exception the validator throws): an **error refuses the run and the save**, a warning is listed and kept (AC-4). The **nozzle, throat, piston and stroke come from the standard sets** (AC-5), and a saved design records **its kind and every input** (AC-6). New `HYDRAULIC_PUMP_DESIGN` and a new `HydraulicPumpModule` that also registers the **five previously unregistered** calculation entities (smoke: 32 modules, 406 entity types). Route `hydraulicpump` (en + ar). 10 unit + 1 opt-in SQLite test; 1000/1000 desktop, 45/45 gateway, build 0 errors, smoke `result: ok`, 69 pages. |
| LFT-07 | Choke analysis | 07 | `Beep.OilandGas.ChokeAnalysis` | Gate met - a choke gives the **rate** (single-phase gas, `GasChokeCalculator`) or the **upstream pressure** a rate needs (the multiphase empirical correlations), and every result names the **flow regime with the ratio that decided it** and **both the correlation chosen and the one actually evaluated** (AC-2). **AC-1 is verified two ways:** the gas path by the throat-area invariant (2x diameter giving 4.00x rate) and each correlation against a pressure recomputed from its **published coefficient set** to 2 decimals (Gilbert 435/0.546/1.89, Ros 17.4/0.5/2.0, Achong 3.82/0.65/1.88, Baxendell 9.56/0.546/1.93). The **erosion threshold always names API RP 14E** (AC-3), the **types and regimes come from the owning enums** (AC-4), the analysis is **deterministic** (AC-5), and a saved result records its **correlation and regime** (AC-6). New `ChokeCorrelationRegistry` in the owner records that **PILEHVARI and SACHDEVA have no model** and fall back to Gilbert, so the row stores CHOSEN = PILEHVARI with USED = GILBERT and a warning. `CHOKE_FLOW_RESULT` gained the analysis columns - entity count stays 406, which is the check that this was a column extension on an already-registered table. Route `choke` (en + ar). 9 unit + 1 opt-in SQLite test; 1010/1010 desktop, 46/46 gateway, build 0 errors, smoke `result: ok`, 70 pages. |
| LFT-08 | Compressor analysis | 07 | `Beep.OilandGas.CompressorAnalysis` | Gate met - a centrifugal or reciprocating duty is checked by the module's own calculators, and the **staging applies the module's interstage temperature and per-stage ratio limits** (AC-2): for a 5 MMscf/d duty from 300 to 900 psia the plan rejects 1 stage (784.8 R) and 2 (686.4 R) against the **660 R** limit and **recommends 3 (648.8 R)**. **AC-1 is verified independently:** the staged head (51,229 ft-lbf/lbm) and motor power (375.8 hp) are recomputed from `n = k*eta/(k*eta-(k-1))`, `H = Z R T/MW * n/(n-1) * (r^((n-1)/n)-1)` and `BHP = W H/33000/eta` with only the engine's Z, agreeing within 1%. **An infeasible machine lists every reason and presents no power** (AC-3) - a fixed single stage at a ratio of 12 gives three reasons. The kinds and analysis tokens come from `CompressorAnalysisWellKnown` (AC-4), **power carries hp and kW** (AC-5), and a saved result records its **kind, staging and efficiency** (AC-6). `COMPRESSOR_POWER_RESULT` gained the analysis columns - entity count stays 406. Route `compressor` (en + ar). 9 unit + 1 opt-in SQLite test; 1020/1020 desktop, 47/47 gateway, build 0 errors, smoke `result: ok`, 71 pages. **Engine fix:** the polytropic exponent `n = k*eta/(k-eta*(k-1))` gave **n = 0.907** for natural gas - compression that cools - in four places. |

| INT-02 | WITSML read, write and store | 17 | `Wellbore.Loaders` (`Witsml*`) | Gate met for what a build can prove - the **1.4.1.1 round-trip**, the **1.x and 2.1 exports**, the **time-log refusal**, the **store states** and the **partial-import counts** are unchanged, and the screen's Store tab now works: **a store query pulls one entity of one wellbore** through `GetFromStoreAsync` and reads it with the same reader a file uses, an **unsupported version is refused naming it**, an **unwritable export folder is refused naming the path**, and the **growing log is shown disabled with its reason**. **AC-2 is structural conformance only** (parses, energyml namespace, uuid, schemaVersion, unique names, references resolve - a tampered reference is caught) because the **Energistics XSDs are not shipped**; that validation is recorded as the owner's item. Credentials stay **session-only**. Route `witsml` (en + ar). 14 unit tests; 1519/1519 desktop, 102/102 gateway, build 0 errors, smoke `result: ok`, 125 pages. |
| INT-03 | OSDU export and ingest | 17 | `Wellbore.Loaders` (`OsduExport`), `DataAI` (`OsduIngest`, `WellReconciliation`) | Gate met for what a build can prove - **AC-2..AC-6 unchanged and passing**, and the screen's remaining behaviour is implemented: the **Export tab selects the record kinds**, an **invalid record refuses the export and is named**, **Send/Retry are offered only when the plan can be sent** with the plan's reasons on the page (which is how the **endpoint-not-configured** state appears), and a **reconciliation is accepted or rejected only by a person**, recorded and audited, **never merged**. **AC-1 is envelope-valid only** because the **OSDU JSON schemas are not shipped** - that validation is the owner's item. Token session-only. Route `osdu` (en + ar). 11 unit tests; 1522/1522 desktop, 102/102 gateway, build 0 errors, smoke `result: ok`, 125 pages. |
| INT-04 | EDM, CSV and XLSX mapping profiles | 17 | `Wellbore.Loaders` (`MappingProfile`, `MappedWellDataProvider`) | Gate met - a **CSV folder and an EDM export both load into the canonical model through a profile** (**AC-1**, **AC-2**); an **unmapped required field fails the test with the entity and field named** (**AC-3**); a **built-in cannot be overwritten and an invalid profile leaves the previous version** (**AC-4**); the **written data is re-read through the same profile before the export is reported as done** (**AC-5**); and the **workbook carries one sheet per entity** (**AC-6**). The profile model, the three sources and both writers are the tools'; `EntityMap.Required` and a multi-sheet `XlsxWriter` were added. Profiles are customer JSON under the data root - **no table is involved**, so the opt-in test drives the service against real files. Route `mappingprofiles` (en + ar). 11 file-based + 1 opt-in test; 1418/1418 desktop, 91/91 gateway, build 0 errors, smoke `result: ok`, 114 pages. |
| INT-05 | Setup session wizard and country environments | 17 | `Wellbore.Setup` (`SetupSession`) | Gate met - a **country preset produces an environment the engine's own validation accepts** (**AC-1**); a **data source is tested and its result recorded with the loader's warnings** (**AC-2**); a **proposal is shown and only written when it is accepted**, and an acceptance the engine does not offer is refused (**AC-3**); **coverage is per source with an explicit unknown when it cannot be computed** (**AC-4**); a **hand-over is refused while a verification case fails** (**AC-5**); and a **failed save leaves the environment byte-identical** (**AC-6**). The wizard is a view over the engine - its steps, tests, coverage, proposals and verification - and edits **the file the application itself loads**, re-reading it after each write. **No table**: the environment is customer data under the data root. Route `wellboresetup` (Interop, en + ar). 5 unit + 1 opt-in test; 1424/1424 desktop, 92/92 gateway, build 0 errors, smoke `result: ok`, 115 pages. |
| INT-06 | Field QC and mapping suggestions | 17 | `Wellbore.Loaders` (`FieldQc`), `Wellbore.Setup`, `DataAI` | Gate met - a **proposal is applied only by the accept command**, never by looking at it (**AC-1**); **coverage read from no well is unknown, never zero** (**AC-2**); a **run cancelled after one well reports that scope and only those findings** (**AC-3**); a **duplicate stays a proposal until a person decides**, and a rejected pair is not proposed again (**AC-4**); **every finding carries the engine's rule, the item and the well** (**AC-5**); and the **export is exactly the grid's filter** (**AC-6**). The run is `FieldQc`'s own; the engine throws on cancellation, so the covered slice is re-run through the same engine; the duplicate decisions are the tools' `WellCrosswalk` beside the environment. **No table**. Route `fieldqc` (Interop, en + ar). 7 unit + 1 opt-in test; 1432/1432 desktop, 93/93 gateway, build 0 errors, smoke `result: ok`, 116 pages. |
| INT-07 | Mapping, gridding and contour maps | 17 | `Mapping` (`WellMaps`, `Gridding`, `Contouring`, `ContourMapView`) | Gate met - a **three-well structure map renders with the legend and scale bar** (**AC-1**); the **projection round-trips within tolerance**, measured in metres at the wells' own latitude (**AC-2**); a **well without coordinates is excluded and listed** with the engine's reason (**AC-3**); the **QC reports empty cells and the maximum extrapolation distance** (**AC-4**); the **contour interval is `NiceInterval` unless overridden** (**AC-5**); and the **PDF is a paginated document with the legend, scale bar and north arrow**, with PNG and SVG beside it (**AC-6**). Every geometric step is the tools'; `mapping.coordinateSystems` ships empty, so the page offers the **UTM zone the wells fall in**. **No table**. Route `map` (Interop, en + ar). 10 unit + 1 opt-in SQLite test over five PPDM tables; 1443/1443 desktop, 94/94 gateway, build 0 errors, smoke `result: ok`, 117 pages. |
| INT-08 | 3D scenes and glTF export | 17 | `Mapping` (`Scene3D`, `Scene3DView`, `Camera3D`, `SceneExport`) | Gate met - a **surface with three wells renders and orbits** (**AC-1**); **picking returns the value at the picked cell**, and a click on nothing returns nothing (**AC-2**); the **vertical exaggeration is applied to the geometry and recorded in the export** as the root extras and its scale (**AC-3**); the **GLB is a glTF 2.0 container** whose JSON parses and whose accessors resolve inside the binary chunk (**AC-4**); the **grid decimation is stated** with the cells, the drawn cells and the stride (**AC-5**); and an **89 401-cell grid is drawn inside the engine's limit and measured** at 444 ms (**AC-6**). The scene, the camera, the drawing and the exporter are the tools'; the picking and the decimation statement are added, and the projection list is INT-07's. **No table**. Route `scene3d` (Interop, en + ar). 8 unit + 1 opt-in SQLite test; 1451/1451 desktop, 95/95 gateway, build 0 errors, smoke `result: ok`, 118 pages. |

Route `courses` (en + ar). 6 unit + 1 opt-in test; 1468/1468 desktop, 97/97 gateway, build 0 errors, smoke `result: ok`, 120 pages. |

The server tracks no sessions, so `TrainingServerOptions` gained an additive `Observe` callback and the page names what it shows. **No table**. Route `trainingserver` (en + ar). 5 unit + 1 opt-in test over a real listener; 1474/1474 desktop, 98/98 gateway, build 0 errors, smoke `result: ok`, 121 pages. |

A **per-user install and uninstall** carry the contract (`install.ps1`, `uninstall.ps1`, `app.manifest` `asInvoker`) - **no path the application resolves is beside the executable, in the install folder, in ProgramData or in the roaming profile during use** (**AC-2**); the **health page checks fourteen assets through the thing that needs each one** and prints what stops working without it (**AC-3**); **removing the data root needs the exact sentence** that names the failure store and the logs (**AC-4**); an **upgrade plan names what it keeps and writes nothing** (**AC-5**); and a **standard user needs no elevation** (**AC-6**). The finding behind it is recorded: the control library resolves **the same semantic palette for all 66 design systems**, so those were **one defect counted 66 times**. AC-1 (0 unnamed, 0 untabbable), AC-4, AC-5 and AC-7 are unchanged and pass; **AC-2** (focus ring) and **AC-6** (200 % scaling) are met as far as a build can show, with the display observations recorded as the owner’s. 2 unit tests in `AccessibilityTests`; 1514/1514 desktop, 102/102 gateway, build 0 errors, smoke `result: ok`, 125 pages. |
| REL-04 | Performance and large-data handling | 18 | - | Gate met - 11 budgets defined and 7 measured (shell, grid page, logs 20k/100k, schematic, startup, 30-min soak 135 MB of 800); decimation ratio, bounded reads with a ceiling, cancel 15-17 ms, `--report-benchmark`, `--soak`, Performance page; 4 budgets need DAT-02/INT-07/INT-08 |
| REL-05 | Release acceptance gates | 18 | none (release process) | Gate met - **thirty items in five areas**, **five computed from artefacts that really exist** (the smoke result, its accessibility counters, its budgets, the project files and the screen sources) and **twenty-five attested with evidence**; an **unproven item is `NotRun`, never a pass**; a **failing check stays failed**; a **broken evidence link fails naming the path**; a **waiver needs a different approver, a reason and a future expiry**, and an **expired waiver reverts to failure**; and the **evidence pack zips the plan's evidence with a manifest** and reports every link it could not fill. Every computed item now passes (REL-03 closed the contrast guard), so the checklist reads **NOT READY TO SHIP on the attested items alone** - which is the honest state. Route `releaseacceptance` (Administration, en + ar). 9 unit + 1 opt-in test; 1514/1514 desktop, 102/102 gateway, build 0 errors, smoke `result: ok`, 125 pages. **Outstanding acceptance items are recorded, not hidden.** |

## 8. Critical path

```
STG-00 ─▶ STG-01 ─▶ STG-02 ─┬─▶ STG-03 ─▶ STG-04 ─▶ STG-05 ─┬─▶ STG-09 ─▶ STG-10 ─▶ STG-18
                            │                                │
                            ├─▶ STG-06 ─▶ STG-07 ─▶ STG-08 ──┘
                            │
                            ├─▶ STG-14 ─▶ STG-11 ─▶ STG-12
                            │
                            └─▶ STG-15 ─▶ STG-16 ─▶ STG-17 ─▶ STG-18
                                                              
STG-13 depends on STG-04 + STG-11
```

**Shortest path to a demonstrable product:** STG-00 → STG-01 → STG-02 → STG-15 → STG-16.
That yields a desktop that signs in, connects to a PPDM database, lists wells, and hosts the
wellbore/log/schematic views with the integrity and drilling toolboxes — the single most
persuasive demonstration of both repositories working together.

---

## 9. Cross-cutting gates

These apply to every stage and are checked once per stage, not per feature.

| Gate | Check | Applies from |
|---|---|---|
| G-BUILD | `dotnet build src/Beep.OilandGas.Desktop.slnx -c Debug` — 0 errors, no new warnings. **From DAT-01 the `Data` project builds BeepDM and `BeepDataSources` from source, so the gate means zero warnings in a `Beep.OilandGas.Desktop.*` path**; sibling-project warnings are attributed, not hidden. | STG-00 |
| G-ANALYZER | No `BEEP0001..0004` diagnostic; `CS8509` clean | STG-00 |
| G-TEST | Selected focused tests for the stage pass; counts recorded | STG-00 |
| G-SMOKE | `--smoke` run completes and writes `smoke-result.txt` with `result: ok` | STG-00 |
| G-STATES | Every new screen implements empty / loading / failed / denied / unsaved | STG-01 |
| G-AUTHZ | Every new page is in `OgModuleCatalog` and the route guard refuses it without entitlement or role | STG-01 |
| G-THEME | No hardcoded colour or font in new XAML; only `DynamicResource` tokens | STG-00 |
| G-I18N | No user-visible literal in code; all strings via `Loc.Get`; RTL layout verified | STG-02 |
| G-A11Y | Keyboard reachable, focus visible, accessible names on all interactive controls | STG-02 |
| G-PERF | Well list ≥ 5 000 rows and a 100 000-row log render within budget (see REL-04) | STG-02 |
| G-FAILURE | No swallowed `catch`; every failure reaches the one pipeline with a reference | STG-00 |
| G-TOAST | Every user-visible failure produces a toast with a reference and no exception text | STG-00 |
| G-AUDIT | Every writing command and every refusal writes exactly one audit row | STG-01 |
| G-NOTOAST | `IOgToastService` is injected only into `OgNotificationHub` | STG-00 |
| G-STORAGE | Every writable path resolves under `OgPaths.DataRoot`, never under the install folder | STG-00 |

---

## 10. Decisions to settle before Stage 0 code

Full detail in [`04-DECISIONS.md`](04-DECISIONS.md). Summary:

| ID | Decision | Default proposal | Blocks |
|---|---|---|---|
| ADR-001 | SkiaSharp unification | Unify on `SkiaSharp 4.154.0` + `Svg.Skia`; retire `SkiaSharp.Svg` | STG-15 |
| ADR-002 | `SkiaSharp.Views.WPF` availability for the unified version | **Accepted change** — do not use it (net4x assets only); render to `WriteableBitmap` | STG-15 |
| ADR-003 | `net10.0-windows` vs `net9.0-windows` | **Accepted** — `net10.0-windows` app; the net9.0 controls library consumes cleanly (probe); suppress CA1416 only on the referencing project before FND-04 | STG-00 |
| ADR-004 | Data access mode default | `ServiceAccessMode.Auto` (remote if configured, else local) | STG-00 |
| ADR-005 | Single `Modules` assembly vs per-area assemblies | Single assembly, folder-per-area | STG-00 |
| ADR-006 | `HeatMap` module inclusion | **Deferred** to post-1.0 (no Web screen parity) | STG-07 |
| ADR-007 | `LeaseAcquisition` surfacing | Add `Lease` area under STG-03 or STG-05 (gap G-01) | STG-03 |
| ADR-011 | One diagnostics pipeline (log + audit + exception) with a toast surface | Adopt `IFailureReporter` + `IOgAuditService` behind one hub; features never call the toast | STG-00 |
| ADR-012 | Toast presentation contract | 5-kind mapping, max 5 stacked, sticky critical, reference always copyable | STG-00 |
| ADR-013 | Failure store target and location | Local SQLite under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\TheTechIdea\BeepOilGas\Diagnostics`; never beside the executable; optional forwarding in `CompanyApi` mode | STG-00 |
| ADR-014 | Local password protection | PBKDF2-SHA256 (210 000 iterations, random salt, constant-time compare); never DPAPI, never plaintext | STG-00 |

## 11. Open gaps

| ID | Gap | Impact | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| G-01 | `Beep.OilandGas.LeaseAcquisition` had no screen group mapped | A whole module would have been unrepresented | **Resolved** by ADR-007: `LSE-01…03` in `STG-03a` |
| G-02 | `HeatMap` has no Web parity screen | Module registered but not surfaced | ADR-006 — deferred; renderer reused by INT-07 |
| G-03 | `Beep.OilandGas.ApiService` has no WPF-facing deployment story (base URL, token, tenant) | Remote mode unproven | Add to `STG-00` FND-02 and `REL-05` |
| G-04 | No `ViewModels` layer exists in either repo | Largest net-new build cost | Accepted; `STG-00` establishes the pattern |
| G-05 | `SkiaSharp` version skew across three dependency groups | Runtime `MissingMethodException` risk | **Resolved** — ADR-001 accepted: SkiaSharp 4.154.0 + Svg.Skia 5.2.3 render proven |

| G-08 | **`Beep.OilandGas.PPDM39.DataManagement` does not compile.** `HSEKPIService` is missing `IHSEKPIService.CountIncidentsAsync(string?, DateTime?, DateTime?)`. `HSE_INCIDENT` has no state column, so "open" depends on the incident workflow. | Blocks DAT-02 and therefore all of STG-01's remaining features and every stage that seeds a module | **Needs the owner's answer**: which record defines an incident as open. | 2026-11-09 | STG-18 | **REL-03 gate met - the 66 contrast failures were one palette counted 66 times, and closing it needed three corrections to my own first attempts.** **The defect:** every one of the 66 control-library skins failed the same pair - **`#F9AB00` on `#FFFBFE` = 1.89:1** where a status colour used as text needs **3:1** - because the library resolves **one semantic palette for all 66 design systems** (its design systems change templates and chrome, not colours). So the number was **one defect counted 66 times**, and one correction fixes all 66. **The fix** is a guard in `OgSkins`: after a skin is applied, `OgThemeContrast.Enforce` moves **only** the colours that fail, **as far as the threshold needs and no further**, keeping the hue - `#F9AB00 -> `#C48700` - and writes them into one dictionary of the application’s own that sits last in the merge chain. **A passing palette is left byte-identical**, so the guard cannot quietly restyle a skin. **Three mistakes of mine, each caught by evidence rather than assumed away:** a first version wrote into the application dictionary, which outranks every merged one, so **each skin after the first audited the previous skin’s adjustment** and 66 failures became **0 with nothing fixed** and an **empty** adjustments list - a false pass, exposed by exactly that empty list; a **foreground-only** adjustment **looped** on white text, which cannot be lightened, so the unit became **the pair** - white stays and **the accent is darkened under it**; and settling the label **before** the accent measured it against the colour it was about to leave. **The smoke run now prints every failure and every colour it moved**, so the next false pass is visible in the record: **`skin-contrast-failures: 0` across 66 skins with 66 named adjustments**. **REL-05 follows honestly:** its computed item 23 now passes, so the release checklist reads **NOT READY TO SHIP on the attested items alone** - and its evidence was corrected rather than left claiming a failure that no longer exists. 2 unit tests; **1514/1514** desktop, **102/102** gateway, build 0 errors, smoke `result: ok` with **125 pages**. **Limits:** the guard corrects **the palette the application draws with**, not the library (another host still gets the failing colours); **template colours are not audited**; a pass is **a floor, not a design review**; and **AC-2 / AC-6 remain display observations** - a focus ring and a reflowing layout are shown, a screen reader and 200 % scaling are not. Evidence: [REL-03](evidence/STG-18-REL-03/observed-evidence.json). |

**857/857** desktop, **32/32** gateway, build 0 errors, smoke `result: ok` with **56 pages**. **Limits:** the work order itself is WRK-01 (not built) - this screen records its reference; the LFT-01 study action and any lift recommendation are not built, and a lift-related method says so; Vogel and DeclineFitter are unused because nothing is computed; the confidence is a stated rule, not a model score; a material variance is neutral evidence and does not by itself flag a well. | 2026-11-06 | STG-18 | **REL-02 gate met — licensing is real, not permissive.** A licence is now a signed envelope: `{schema, keyId, payload(base64url), signature(base64url)}` with RSA-2048/SHA-256 over the payload's **exact bytes** (no serializer in the trust path) verified against an **embedded** public key. `--licence-check` verifies and names the key: signed sample exit 0 `verified-ok (key dev-2026)`; altered signature exit 2 `licence-signature-invalid`; unsigned exit 2 `licence-unsigned`. `OgEntitlement` is the second gate: a module the licence does not grant is denied **even for a system administrator**, and an entitled module still needs the role. Expired past grace + enforced = **reads continue, writes blocked**, core never blocked; inside grace everything works and the days are counted. `Import` refuses anything whose signature does not verify and leaves the installed licence working, then re-reads and raises `Changed` so the shell rebuilds navigation and banner without a restart (AC-6). Licence page added (route `licence`) and a shell banner for expired/grace/unlicensed-under-enforcement. **Security:** the repo carries a **development** key pair — public half embedded, private half a test fixture only; guard `No_private_key_material_is_committed_under_src` fails the build if any file under `src` holds private-key material, and the production vendor key must replace `licence-keys.json` before release (ADR-015). Three headless licence tests were rewritten because they encoded the old "unsigned is fine" behaviour. 299/299 tests, build 0/0, smoke `licence-imported True`, `licence-signature-verified True`, `licence-tampered-refused True`, `licence-expired-write-blocked True`, `result: ok`. ## 12a. Setup lifecycle (SETUP-01 … SETUP-13)

The setup plan lives in `plan/setup/`. It is a revision of the setup chapter implied by FND-07, written after
researching how corporate applications actually provision a company. The full rationale, the research extract and
the gap register are in [SETUP-00](setup/00-SETUP-LIFECYCLE.md).

**The rule that shapes every stage:** the only fact on a workstation is the connection that reaches the database.
The deployment, the company record, the settings, the RBAC, the module set and the environment are rows in that
database and are written by the SystemAdmin. **No setting is stored in a file** — `setup.json` and the local
credential file were removed; a personal installation is simply one whose database is a local file. A setting is
never read by an unauthenticated caller.

| ID | Stage | Actor | Status | Document |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SETUP-01 | Deployment choice (machine) | Installer | Built | [doc](setup/SETUP-01-DEPLOYMENT.md) |
| SETUP-02 | Database connection (machine) | Installer | Built (AC-7 blocked on an enterprise server) | [doc](setup/SETUP-02-CONNECTION.md) |
| SETUP-03 | Company creation and the first SystemAdmin | Installer → SystemAdmin | Built | [doc](setup/SETUP-03-COMPANY-CREATION.md) |
| SETUP-04 | Company settings record | SystemAdmin | Built | [doc](setup/SETUP-04-COMPANY-SETTINGS.md) |
| SETUP-05 | RBAC: roles, permissions, users | SystemAdmin | Built | [doc](setup/SETUP-05-RBAC.md) |
| SETUP-06 | Modules, reference data and LOV | SystemAdmin | Built | [doc](setup/SETUP-06-MODULES-LOV.md) |
| SETUP-07 | Shared Oil & Gas environment | SystemAdmin | Built | [doc](setup/SETUP-07-ENVIRONMENT.md) |
| SETUP-08 | Joining an existing company | Installer + User | Built | [doc](setup/SETUP-08-JOIN-COMPANY.md) |
| SETUP-09 | Every start: authenticate, then read settings | User | Built | [doc](setup/SETUP-09-DAILY-LOGIN.md) |
| SETUP-10 | Admin change control | SystemAdmin | Built | [doc](setup/SETUP-10-ADMIN-CHANGE.md) |
| SETUP-11 | Upgrade and migration | SystemAdmin | Built | [doc](setup/SETUP-11-UPGRADE.md) |
| SETUP-12 | Backup, restore and an unreachable database | SystemAdmin + DBA | Built | [doc](setup/SETUP-12-BACKUP-RESTORE-OFFLINE.md) |
| SETUP-13 | Personal installation | Installer | Built | [doc](setup/SETUP-13-PERSONAL.md) |

**Research sources (read directly, extracts in the evidence file):**

| Source | The fact taken |
|---|---|
| OWASP Authentication Cheat Sheet | length 15 without MFA / 64 maximum / no composition / no forced rotation / generic refusals / log failures / no backend account as a login |
| OWASP Multi-Tenant Security Cheat Sheet | tenant context from a verified identity; onboarding all-or-nothing; credentials hashed; restore is tenant-scoped |
| AWS Well-Architected SaaS Lens (tenant onboarding) | one orchestrator; tenant id + settings + tenant admin created together; observable; offboarding designed with onboarding |
| Keycloak bootstrap admin | the bootstrap administrator is temporary and there is a recovery path |
| NN/g "Wizards" | ordered steps, step list with the current step, descriptive labels, exit and resume |

**Gap register:** **G-9 is blocked, not open in code** — there is no enterprise SQL Server/PostgreSQL/Oracle in this
environment, so SETUP-02 AC-7 (a server connection exercised against a real server) is recorded as blocked and is
not claimed as proved. **Every other gap is closed:** G-1 (step rail and resume), G-2 (company rollback), G-3
(provisional administrator), G-4 (user management), G-5 (permission checks), G-6 (breached-password list), G-7
(backup/restore), G-8 (document migration), and SETUP-05 AC-7 (lockout). No stage is "planned" any more.

## 13. Versioning and maintenance

- Version this document with every stage gate change.
- Update §5 and §6 status columns in the same edit as the feature document.
- Never delete a row; mark `Deferred` with a reason.
- The tracker is authoritative over any status claim in a feature document.
